Title: CrossFit Open 2026 Athlete Dataset
Date: 2026-07-03
Category: Fitness
Tags: crossfit, fitness, data, athlete
Slug: crossfit-open-2026-athlete-dataset
Authors: François Leblanc
Summary: A compact public 2026 CrossFit Open dataset release, with percentile cutoffs and benchmark target estimates by sex and age-group division.

<div class="cf-open-article"><div class="cf-hero"><strong>What this page answers:</strong> choose a division and percentile, then see the exact 2026 Open rank cutoff, boundary Open scores, and a target-builder for the benchmark numbers people actually ask about: FTP, 400m run, 1RM clean &amp; jerk, and CrossFit Total.</div><div class="cf-warning"><strong>Caveat first:</strong> the leaderboard percentile cutoffs are solid because they come from the full 2026 Open crawl. The benchmark targets are not full-population measurements yet. CrossFit profile benchmarks are self-reported, often lifetime PRs rather than current fitness, and the compact release only includes 165 sampled benchmark rows. The target-builder labels each output as observed, modelled, or insufficient instead of pretending all four benchmarks are equally measured.</div><div class="cf-grid"><div class="cf-card"><h3>Divisions</h3><div class="cf-big">23</div><div class="cf-small">including teams; non-team divisions power the explorer</div></div><div class="cf-card"><h3>Athletes</h3><div class="cf-big">254,531</div><div class="cf-small">unique public leaderboard athletes</div></div><div class="cf-card"><h3>Leaderboard entries</h3><div class="cf-big">388,423</div><div class="cf-small">athlete-by-division results</div></div><div class="cf-card"><h3>Workout scores</h3><div class="cf-big">1,165,269</div><div class="cf-small">three Open workout rows per entry where present</div></div></div></div>

<div class="cf-open-article">
<h2>Percentile Target Explorer</h2>
<p>This is the main interface. The rank and workout outputs are exact from the leaderboard. The benchmark cards are an honest training-target layer on top of the data: observed where the sampled profiles support it, modelled where the page needs to bridge from leaderboard percentile to broader fitness qualities.</p>
<div class="cf-controls">
<div>
<label for="cfDivision">Division</label>
<select id="cfDivision">
</select>
</div>
<div>
<label for="cfPercentile">Percentile: <span id="cfPercentileLabel">90</span>th</label>
<input id="cfPercentile" type="range" min="50" max="99" step="1" value="90">
</div>
<div>
<label for="cfBodyweight">Bodyweight for modelled targets (kg)</label>
<input id="cfBodyweight" type="number" min="45" max="140" step="1" value="88">
</div>
</div>
<div class="cf-grid" id="cfLeaderboardCards">
</div>
<div class="cf-targets" id="cfBenchmarkCards">
</div>
<div class="cf-small" id="cfExplorerNote">
</div>
<h2>Rank Cutoffs, Faceted</h2>
<p>These bars show the allowed rank cutoff for selected divisions. Longer bars mean a larger absolute field: the 90th percentile in Men still allows thousands of athletes, while the same percentile in smaller age groups allows far fewer.</p>

<h2>Published Profile PR Sample</h2>
<p>The sample below is deliberately labelled as a sample, not a population percentile table. It is mostly elite men and Men 35-39 athletes, so it is useful as a sanity check for high-end published PRs, not as a complete answer for every Open percentile.</p>
<div class="cf-bar-panel">
<div class="cf-bar-row">

preferred=['Men 35-39','Men','Women','Women 35-39','Men 40-44','Women 40-44'];[...data.divisions].sort((a,b)=>{const ai=preferred.indexOf(a.name),bi=preferred.indexOf(b.name);if(ai!==-1||bi!==-1)return(ai===-1?99:ai)-(bi===-1?99:bi);return a.name.localeCompare(b.name)}).forEach(d=>{const o=document.createElement('option');o.value=d.id;o.textContent=`${d.name} (${d.athletes.toLocaleString()} athletes)`;if(d.name==='Men 35-39')o.selected=true;divisionSelect.appendChild(o)});function interp(p,a){const pts=Object.keys(a).map(Number).sort((x,y)=>x-y);if(p<=pts[0])return a[pts[0]];if(p>=pts.at(-1))return a[pts.at(-1)];for(let j=0;j<pts.length-1;j++){const lo=pts[j],hi=pts[j+1];if(p>=lo&&p<=hi){const t=(p-lo)/(hi-lo);return a[lo]+t*(a[hi]-a[lo])}}}function time(sec){sec=Math.round(sec);return `${Math.floor(sec/60)}:${String(sec%60).padStart(2,'0')}`}function med(v){const x=[...v].sort((a,b)=>a-b);if(!x.length)return null;const m=Math.floor(x.length/2);return x.length%2?x[m]:(x[m-1]+x[m])/2}function obs(div,p,stat){const s=data.benchSamples.filter(x=>String(x.divisionId)===String(div)&&x.stat===stat&&x.leaderboardPercentile>=p);if(s.length<3)return null;return{value:med(s.map(x=>x.value)),n:s.length,metric:s[0].metric,lower:s[0].better==='lower'}}function card(t,v,d,cls,src){return `<div class="cf-target-card"><h4>${t}</h4><div class="cf-target-value">${v}</div><p>${d}</p><span class="cf-source ${cls||''}">${src}</span></div>`}function update(){const div=divisionSelect.value,p=Number(percentileInput.value),bw=Number(bodyweightInput.value||88);percentileLabel.textContent=p;const division=data.divisions.find(d=>String(d.id)===String(div)),cutoff=data.cutoffs.find(r=>String(r.divisionId)===String(div)&&Number(r.percentile)===p);if(!cutoff)return;leaderboardCards.innerHTML=[card('Rank cutoff',`#${cutoff.rank.toLocaleString()}`,`${cutoff.athletesAtOrAbove.toLocaleString()} athletes at or above the ${p}th percentile.`,'','exact leaderboard'),card('Overall score',cutoff.overallScore||'n/a',`Boundary athlete: ${cutoff.athleteName||'not published'}.`,'','exact leaderboard'),card('Workout 1',cutoff.workout1||'n/a','Boundary score at this this percentile.','','exact leaderboard')].join('');const sex=division.sex==='F'?'F':'M',ftp=sex==='F'?{50:2.4,75:3.0,90:3.6,95:4.0,99:4.6}:{50:2.8,75:3.4,90:4.0,95:4.4,99:5.0},run=sex==='F'?{50:110,75:92,90:78,95:70,99:62}:{50:95,75:80,90:68,95:62,99:55},cj=sex==='F'?{50:.75,75:.95,90:1.15,95:1.30,99:1.55}:{50:1.0,75:1.2,90:1.45,95:1.65,99:1.9},cft=sex==='F'?{50:2.8,75:3.6,90:4.5,95:5.2,99:6.0}:{50:3.5,75:4.5,90:5.5,95:6.2,99:7.0};const ocj=obs(div,p,'Clean & Jerk 1RM'),orun=obs(div,p,'Run 400m'),ocft=obs(div,p,'CrossFit Total'),ftpWkg=interp(p,ftp),cjKg=ocj?ocj.value:interp(p,cj)*bw,runSec=orun?orun.value:interp(p,run),cftKg=ocft?ocft.value:interp(p,cft)*bw;benchmarkCards.innerHTML=[card('FTP',`${Math.round(ftpWkg*bw)} W`,`${ftpWkg.toFixed(1)} W/kg at ${bw} kg. Not present in CrossFit profiles; editable model target.`,'model','modelled'),card('400m run',time(runSec),orun?`Median of ${orun.n} sampled public profile PRs at/above this leaderboard percentile.`:'Modelled because sampled public profiles are too sparse for this division/percentile.',orun?'':'model',orun?'observed sample':'modelled'),card('Clean & jerk 1RM',`${Math.round(cjKg)} kg`,ocj?`Median of ${ocj.n} sampled public profile PRs at/above this leaderboard percentile.`:`${(cjKg/bw).toFixed(2)}x bodyweight model because observed profile PRs are too sparse.`,ocj?'':'model',ocj?'observed sample':'modelled'),card('CrossFit Total',`${Math.round(cftKg)} kg`,ocft?`Back squat + strict press + deadlift from ${ocft.n} sampled profiles.`:`${(cftKg/bw).toFixed(1)}x bodyweight model; direct published CFT samples are insufficient.`,ocft?'':'model',ocft?'observed sample':'modelled')].join('');note.textContent='Benchmark caveat: self-reported profile values are usually lifetime PRs, not current-state fitness. For robust benchmark percentiles, the missing next step is a targeted crawl of public profiles around each cutoff rank.'}divisionSelect.addEventListener('change',update);percentileInput.addEventListener('input',update);bodyweightInput.addEventListener('input',update);update()})();

<h2>Dataset Release</h2>
<p>The dataset is published as a compact normalized GitHub Release asset rather than tracked directly in the repository. The release excludes raw API response bodies and the checkpoint SQLite database.</p>

<div class="cf-open-article">
<h2>What Is Still Missing</h2>
<p>The current article can answer leaderboard percentile questions well. It cannot yet honestly answer “what is the true 75th percentile clean &amp; jerk PR among Men 35-39 Open athletes?” because the compact release only has a small, skewed profile benchmark sample.</p>
<p>The right next dataset pass is targeted and finite: for each sex/division and selected percentile band, fetch public profiles for athletes around the cutoff rank, parse benchmark stats, and publish a second benchmark-focused release table with enough observations per stat to report n, median, 25th/75th, and missingness rate. That would turn the target-builder’s modelled cards into observed cards.</p>
</div>
